docs/evidence/backlog.md:483
 481  | M100 | **A failed start must be visible** | `app.whenReady()`'s catch does `console.error` then `app.quit()` — so when bootstrap fails the operator double-clicks the app and **nothing happens**: no window, no dialog, no explanation, and `console.error` in a packaged Electron app goes to a log nobody knows exists. Three distinct causes reach it: the `supabase` CLI is not on PATH, the stack will not start, or the migrations were never applied. All three produce the same silence. **The product's first impression, when anything is wrong, is that it does not work at all.** Needs a real dialog naming which precondition failed and what to do | **shipped** — the catch now classifies, writes a log the operator can find, and shows a dialog naming the precondition with what to do about it. **The row said three causes; measuring found seven**, and two pairs that a careless matcher conflates: a missing binary and a start that timed out are both `spawnSync supabase <CODE>`, and a missing WORKING DIRECTORY is `ENOENT` exactly like a missing binary — so a wrong `FABRIC_REPO` would have told the operator to install software they already have. That ambiguity is removed in `main/repoRoot.ts`, where the knowledge to resolve it exists. Retry is offered only before `surface.start()`, a boundary OBSERVED rather than inferred. Driven for real three times against a rebuilt bundle |
 482  | M101 | **Starting the stack blocks the main thread** | `startStack()` is `execFileSync` with a 240 s timeout, called straight after `splashWindow(...)` is created. Electron's main thread is the browser process message loop, so for as long as it blocks nothing is pumped. **Whether the splash paints its text before the block lands is UNVERIFIED** — an Electron probe to settle it hung and was abandoned at three attempts, per the rabbit-hole rule. If it does not paint, a person watches a 380×120 rectangle for up to four minutes. Move the start to a child process and poll, or prove the paint lands | **shipped** — and the UNVERIFIED half is settled first, which changes the answer: **the splash did not paint**. Measured from inside the renderer, whose own clock puts its script at +6387 ms against a block ending at +6328 ms; a main-process timestamp could not have shown it, because an event fired during a block is merely delivered late. The abandoned probe was abandoned for the right reason and the bounded version took one run. `startStack`, `stackStatusEnv` and `resolveSupabaseEnv` are now asynchronous, the same probe shows the page running 44 ms into the wait, and the splash carries elapsed time and the stack’s own last line. Moving the call also changed what a timeout LOOKS like, which would have silently downgraded M100’s diagnosis — caught and covered |
 483  | M102 | **Six loaders fire on every feed tick** | `ProjectHome.tsx` hangs `loadRepos`, `loadStats`, `loadClaims`, `loadTranscripts`, `loadRepoStates` and `loadQuota` off one effect whose deps include `feedMark`. The feed polls every 2 s, so while any session is producing events **all six go over IPC every two seconds** — including a transcript query returning up to 30 rows with excerpts. **M42 caused this**: replacing `feed.length` with a monotonic mark cured the freeze at the 500-event cap and, in the same edit, turned it into a stampede. The fix is to split the effect by what each loader actually depends on — repositories on `config_revision`, claims and statistics on events, transcripts only when a session ends, quota on its own 120 s clock | proposed |
 484  | M113 | **Three milestones say shipped and mean half-shipped** | audit found the board more generous than the ledger. **M83 "Quota on the home"** — the home is `EstateHome.tsx` and it renders no quota at all; the only quota on screen is the PROJECT page, and `quota.byModel` is computed and rendered nowhere. **M56** claims "last commit and its age per attached repository" — `lastCommit` is read and unit-tested and rendered nowhere, and the bar shows `repos[0]` plus a count. **M57** claims lines of code, commits, changed lines, tokens and cost — none of the five ship. In every case `verification.md` is honest (it verifies the READER) and the board is not, and the board is what someone acts on | **shipped** — all three claims MEASURED TRUE first, unlike M106’s: `EstateHome` had no quota and `byModel` rendered in zero files; `lastCommit` appeared only in `types.ts` and `main/`; `ProjectStats` held only storage facts. M57 ships what is measurable — commits and the lines they moved, over a window carried WITH them — and its row is corrected for what is not: “lines of code” is not shipped under that name because the honest count is a third data files, and tokens and cost are not shippable at all per CO-096 |
 485  | M114 | **Two register id spaces leak** | **M80 does not exist** — ids run M0–M112 with exactly one hole. And eight four-digit `CO-00NN` references resolve against nothing, in `adr/0020`, `passioncode-platform.md` and inside CO-049's own status — **the exact trap CO-053 documents**, reproduced in the documents instead of the tool | **shipped** — the M-space half closed when the commit-scope gate found `M146`, an id ten commits used and no row held. The CO half is closed here: eight four-digit references corrected to the form the register uses, and a gate that fails on the form. The M80 hole stays a hole — an id nobody used is not a defect, and renumbering to close it would move every reference to every row after it |
 486  | M115 | **"suite now N probes" is a number with no referent** | `verification.md` runs 11 → 15 → 19 → 22 → 25 → 29 → 33. The suite has 18 numbered probes and 58 assertion sites; neither is 33. The increments also skip probes the same ledger introduces — P14 exists and never got one, P16 has seven assertion sites and never got one. **And the counter has a blind spot found 2026-09-03:** the gate matches `^\| [A-Z]{2,3}-REQ-`, so the seven `BRAND-REQ-*` rows are invisible to it — `verification.md` holds more rows than any number quoted about it | **shipped** — the counter’s blind spot was widened to a six-letter prefix when writing `FEED-REQ` made the total refuse to move, and the suite’s size is now a number `check-registers.mjs` produces. The seven historical tallies stay and are LABELLED as historical: each was true at its own merge. No assertion count is published, because the source has 95 call sites and a green run prints 65 lines and a number nobody can define is the thing this row is about |

 488  **Fixed during this audit:** `splashWindow` had no `backgroundColor`. M63 shipped as
docs/evidence/backlog.md:515
 513  | id | Milestone | What it is | Status |
 514  |---|---|---|---|
 515  | M103 | **Instruction delivery is guesswork twice over** | `index.ts` writes `` `${instruction}\r` `` into the PTY, and the field is a `textarea rows={3}` where Enter inserts a newline **by design**. **Every embedded newline is a separate Enter**, so the first two-paragraph brief runs as several commands. Beside it, the write is scheduled by a bare `setTimeout(…, 1200)` — an unguarded, uncancellable guess that the agent's TUI is accepting input. On a cold start it types into a half-drawn screen while the task row already says "running". **This is the product's primary action.** Send one bracketed-paste block, and wait for a readiness marker rather than a clock | **shipped** — `cf0e704`: the instruction reaches a listening agent, whole and once |
 516
 517  ### Group B — the agent surface's request lifecycle
 518
 519  | id | Milestone | What it is | Status |
 520  |---|---|---|---|
docs/evidence/backlog.md:527
 525  | id | Milestone | What it is | Status |
 526  |---|---|---|---|
 527  | M105 | **Every byte an agent prints costs a full-buffer regex and a blocking write** | `pty.onData` does a synchronous `writeSync`, then a regex + split + map + filter over the whole 400 000-char scrollback — to compute one 120-character `tail`. The same shape ships that scrollback over IPC on every 3 s poll for **all** sessions, so three undismissed sessions serialise ~1.2 MB out of the main process every three seconds so a tab can say "2 live". The main process is also the MCP server, the journal writer and the IPC broker for four windows. And the tail's regex is missing the `ESC`, so it leaves `\x1b` in and eats ordinary text (`[link]` → `ink]`) while the correct pattern sits two files away in `transcripts.ts` | proposed |

 529  ### Group D — failures that reach nobody
 530
 531  | id | Milestone | What it is | Status |
 532  |---|---|---|---|
docs/evidence/backlog.md:547
 545  | id | Milestone | What it is | Status |
 546  |---|---|---|---|
 547  | M109 | **The IPC contract is type-checked on one side only, and has drifted** | `preload` types the calls; nothing connects a handler to its declared return, so `tsc` compares nothing. Three live drifts found: `files.openExternally` is declared `Promise<void>` and `shell.openPath` resolves to **an error string** that the caller discards, so a failure is indistinguishable from success; `feed.replay` is declared `FeedEvent[]` and returns wider rows; `RepoState.error` is produced and rendered by nothing, so a repo whose git call failed shows its stale branch with full confidence | **partly shipped** — all three named drifts are fixed: a failed `openExternally` is reported and shown, a failed git read is no longer rendered as a branch, and the feed channel narrows to the shape it declares. The MECHANISM exists too — `Returns<FabricApi[…]>` derives a handler’s return type from the one declaration, so `tsc` compares them. **It is applied to three handlers and about forty are unannotated**, which is a mechanical sweep this change does not make |
 548  | M110 | **No renderer tests, no CI, and six suites go green having run nothing** | `grep -riE 'jsdom\|vitest\|testing-library\|playwright'` across every `package.json` returns **nothing** — 2 800 lines of TSX with no seam. `index.ts` is unimportable (`app.whenReady()` at module scope), so `bootstrap`, `registerIpc`, all ~35 handlers, `reconcileOpenTasks` and `captureTranscript` are reachable only by launching Electron — `reconcile.test.mjs` says so in its own header and **re-implements the query instead of calling it**. And **6 of 12 desktop suites `process.exit(0)` when the local stack is down**, with no `.github/workflows` anywhere: `pnpm test` prints success on any machine where the database half ran zero assertions | **partly shipped** — the renderer has tests and CI runs as a script (`scripts/ci.sh`), and the ROUTINE TICK — the one path that launches an agent with nobody watching — was extracted from `index.ts` so a probe can drive it. The rest of `index.ts` is still reachable only by launching Electron, and "the file is unimportable" is recorded as a fact about the file rather than a reason |
 549  | M111 | **Cmd+W destroys every tab, and tabs are never persisted** | no `Menu` is installed, so Electron's default binds Cmd+W to Close Window, and `tabs` is plain component state. Five projects open, one reflex keystroke, the whole working set is gone with no undo and no restore on relaunch | **shipped** — Cmd+W closes the tab and Cmd+Shift+W the window, through a menu built from Electron’s ROLES so copy, paste and quit survive being replaced. The working set is written on every change and restored once the projects are known; a tab whose project is gone is dropped and said, and a draft is never restored because the tab without its content claims work that is not there |

docs/evidence/backlog.md:573
 571  | M116 | **The mark reaches the application** | the passion-fruit family ships in `assets/brand/favicon/` and `assets/brand/brand-pack/` with a manifest and two build scripts, and **nothing consumes it**: `electron-builder.yml` names no `icon`, no `BrowserWindow` is constructed with one, and there is no dev-mode dock icon. The packaged app therefore wears the stock Electron icon on the operator's own dock. Needs the raster the packager wants, the builder key, the dev dock call, the window icon for the platforms that read it, and a check that fails when the icon file and the manifest's checksum disagree | **shipped 2026-09-03** |
 572  | M117 | **The interface is designed IN the pack, not merely near it** | `docs/brand/ui.md` was accepted 2026-09-02 and states the rules — monochrome controls, one chromatic brand object, state carried by its word, `--app-*` aliases only, the six-step space ladder. What does not exist is the **component layer those rules describe**: the renderer has screens, not components, so every new screen re-decides what a card, a state chip, an empty state, a toolbar and a table row are. Needs the core set extracted and documented against the pack, the existing screens moved onto it, and the design gate extended from "no raw colour" to "no component invented outside the set". Absorbs M64 | **shipped 2026-09-05** |
 573  | M118 | **The workspace is a repository, offered at initialisation** | initialising a workspace offers git immediately. Settings, the project records, and the relations between them live in that repository, so the operator's estate is a thing they can read, diff and carry to another machine. An existing workspace repository may be named at initialisation and imported from instead of created. **Declining git is a supported answer, not a failure**: the workspace stays local, and the product says plainly that it then has no backup and no version history. Reconciles with ADR-0002 — declared data is mirrored to git, observed data is not — which decides WHAT may go in it | **shipped** — ADR-0002’s mirror, and now the question that offers it: choose a folder, adopt an existing workspace, or decline. No dismiss, because declining IS later and is reversible. An import preserves IDS — that is what makes it the same estate carried to another machine rather than a copy — refuses an estate that already holds projects, and refuses a file it cannot read whole. Fabric writes and initialises the repository; committing stays the operator’s |
 574  | M119 | **The CEO proposes the projects from the evidence** | on a fresh workspace the CEO agent reads what is there and proposes projects — a name, a folder or repository, a short summary, a description carrying what it found. The operator accepts, edits or rejects each. **This is a proposal, never an import**: a project the operator never confirmed must not appear as one they own | proposed |
 575  | M120 | **Favourites — at most five** | when the proposal produces many projects, the operator picks up to five as favourites and the home leads with them. **Skipped entirely at six projects or fewer**, because a ranking of five out of six is ceremony | **shipped** — `242ebce`: favourites are a partition of the project list, and the five-cap is enforced where the write happens. **Returns at M38**, where a favourite becomes a `(person_id, project_id)` row rather than operator-local state |
 576  | M121 | **Shortcuts instead of a blank prompt** | a project offers named starting actions beside the free-text field — audit this project, known bugs, errors in production, continue from the backlog. They are content, not code: the preset mechanism already exists (`TaskPreset` — a row in the string registry), so this is a set of presets plus the queries behind the ones that read real data | **shipped** — two constant presets and two that READ the project, offered only when their source has something in it and carrying the items so the session does not open by rediscovering them. A truncated list keeps the true count and says what it left out. **"Errors in production" is deliberately absent**: nothing observes production yet (M129), and a shortcut for a signal we do not collect is a promise the product cannot keep |
 577  | M122 | **A kanban board per project, written by agents** | tasks a session produces are picked up and land on the board without anyone retyping them. The board is per project. **The load-bearing consequence:** the board becomes the place task state lives, so M53's list, M79's backlog state and M54's detail are its views rather than three separate screens | **shipped** with M146 — the board is per project, agents write to it through the task tools, and M53’s list, M79’s backlog state and M54’s detail are its views rather than three screens |
 578  | M123 | **Task management is a system skill in every agent's routing** | agents create and update tasks through one installed skill that is mandatory in the routing of every agent Fabric launches — the same shape as the other system skills. This is the channel M122 depends on, and it is what makes the board agent-written rather than agent-adjacent. Needs the skill itself, its installation into each admitted runner, and the decision of what it speaks to Fabric — the agent surface already has the door | **shipped** — the contract (seven `fabric_task_*` tools) and now the DELIVERY: a preamble that names `fabric_whoami` and no rule, carried by the adapter that can carry it, and `session.oriented@1` journalled so a session that never read its rules is visible rather than assumed |
docs/evidence/backlog.md:614
 612  | M138 | **The four authority event types are registered** | `policy.decided@1`, `grant.issued@1`, `grant.consumed@1`, `effect.executed@1`. They were named in the slice-3 register and never registered, so `append_event` refused them — the register working rather than failing | **shipped 2026-09-03** |
 613  | M139 | **A floored action in the product** | **not project deletion, which does not exist — the forced save, which does.** `writeFile(force)` overwrites content that changed on disk, which may be an agent's work written seconds ago, and until now the only thing between that and silent loss was a banner: no event, no authority, no receipt. The editor now asks for a grant naming THAT FILE when the operator chooses to overwrite, policy decides, and the write happens only if it says so. A refusal reaches the operator in policy's own words rather than as a raw IPC exception (M106's shape) | **shipped 2026-09-03** |
 614  | M140 | **The agent asks, the operator grants** | today only the operator can hold authority. This is the row that turns the plane from a control the operator uses into the boundary agents work inside | **partly shipped** — `c5bf512`: `fabric_effect_request` presents nothing and the surface finds the grant, so the agent never holds the permission it is asking for. **The row named `canUseTool` and this is not that, which matters:** the hook would intercept EVERY tool call, and the tool intercepts only what the agent chooses to ask about. An agent that does not ask is not stopped. Closing that needs the runner’s own permission hook, which is per-runner and not scheduled |

 641  | M155 | **The protocol is observed where it cannot be refused** | Three tiers, and naming them is the point: REFUSED (closing your own task, writing without a grant, handing off with no named thing, the loop bound), OBSERVED (rules never read, a review with no brief, a lease left held at session end), and ADVICE (the quality of a question or an insight, which nothing here can judge).  642  | M156 | **A project's priority is declared, and pressure is measured beside it** | Tier — `critical` · `active` · `steady` · `paused` — is the operator's claim and is **never mutated by the system**; pressure (blocked tasks, oldest open question, a goal due within the week, recent activity) is measured and CAPPED, so it can lift a `steady` project above an idle `active` one and never above `critical`. Letting a busy project promote itself destroys the claim-versus-observation signal that makes the tier worth having (ADR-0002, ADR-0036). Storage: `priority_tier`/`priority_because`/`ceo_trust` on the project (tier NEVER mutated by the system — ADR-0002), one `estate_settings` row, `apply_priority` projector. P23 round-trips a declaration touching nothing else, a bad tier refused by the check, an override cleared, and the settings upsert. Four defects planted and caught |

 637  | M151 | **The Board — top 5 on the home, top 10 in a project, all of it on its own screen** | One query over the ranked union of derived obligations and open questions, at three scopes. The project cards count from the SAME query (M147's rule). It is a QUERY and not a table: a stored board is a second copy of the truth, and the copy that drifts is the one the operator is reading | proposed |
 638  | M152 | **Answering closes the loop, and the answer is a decision** | The operator answers; the question closes, its blocked tasks unblock, and **the answer is written to project memory as a `decision` fact**. That one choice needs no delivery code: the context pack already carries current facts into every session, `decisions.ts` already models supersession, and `digest.ts` already reads decisions. This is the step at which the product does what the whole design is for | proposed |
 639  | M153 | **The CEO routine — withdraw the stale, de-duplicate, escalate** | Runs on the routine tick and holds no work node. A question whose blocked work was cancelled is withdrawn WITH ITS REASON rather than deleted or left open; age never expires a question, it only raises it. Exact de-duplication only — semantic merging is named as waiting for a model rather than half-built | proposed |
 640  | M154 | **Findings and traps get a reader, so writing one stops being shouting into a drawer** | `memory_facts.kind` already carries `finding` and `trap` and `fabric_memory_remember` already writes them. No new store: two readers instead — the context pack, which already carries them to the next session, and a retro surface grouping them by the task that produced them, where a wrong one can be superseded | proposed |
 641  | M155 | **The protocol is observed where it cannot be refused** | Three tiers, and naming them is the point: REFUSED (closing your own task, writing without a grant, handing off with no named thing, the loop bound), OBSERVED (rules never read, a review with no brief, a lease left held at session end), and ADVICE (the quality of a question or an insight, which nothing here can judge). Blocking on the unenforceable third is how a protocol becomes a workaround factory | proposed |
 642  | M156 | **A project's priority is declared, and pressure is measured beside it** | Tier — `critical` · `active` · `steady` · `paused` — is the operator's claim and is **never mutated by the system**; pressure (blocked tasks, oldest open question, a goal due within the week, recent activity) is measured and CAPPED, so it can lift a `steady` project above an idle `active` one and never above `critical`. Letting a busy project promote itself destroys the claim-versus-observation signal that makes the tier worth having (ADR-0002, ADR-0036). Plus `ceo_trust` on the estate with a per-project override that **may never exceed it** — ADR-0004's inheritance rule, not a second scale | **shipped** — `projectWeight` as a tested pure module: **tier + capped pressure**, where the cap is forced to (15,30) so a maxed `steady` project outranks an idle `active` one and never a bare `critical` one — a flat cap outside that window cannot satisfy both, which a test found before the code did. `ceoTrustFor` inherits the estate and clamps an override DOWN (ADR-0004 shape); an unknown level fails closed to `ask`. Storage: `priority_tier`/`priority_because`/`ceo_trust` on the project (tier NEVER mutated by the system — ADR-0002), one `estate_settings` row, `apply_priority` projector. P23 round-trips a declaration touching nothing else, a bad tier refused by the check, an override cleared, and the settings upsert. Four defects planted and caught |
 643  | M157 | **The CEO settles what it can CITE, and every settlement is visible and reversible** | A question carries `about` — a stable key naming its subject — and a decision fact with the same key answers it mechanically. That is the whole mechanism by which a CEO with no model answers anything. **A settlement with no basis is refused at every trust level**: an answer the CEO cannot cite is an answer it invented. Safety is citation, a standing “settled by the CEO” list, and one-press override — never a daily confirmation, which is a tax that trains the operator not to read | proposed |

 643  | M157 | **The CEO settles what it can CITE, and every settlement is visible and reversible** | A question carries `about` — a stable key naming its subject — and a decision fact with the same key answers it mechanically. That is the whole mechanism by which a CEO with no model answers anything. **A settlement with no basis is refused at every trust level**: an answer the CEO cannot cite is an answer it invented. Safety is citation, a standing “settled by the CEO” list, and one-press override — never a daily confirmation, which is a tax that trains the operator not to read | proposed |
 644  | M158 | **A decision that changes is scored, and the operator's own word is held** | `criticality = 40×(replaces a PERSON's decision) + 30×(needs a grant) + 20×(already released) + min(5×dependents,20) + project weight`. Above the threshold it becomes an `approval` question carrying the original, the replacement and the components. The first term puts any change to the operator's own decision beyond the CEO at any trust level — a decision the estate can overwrite is not a decision. A decision the CEO made carries no such term: the estate may revise its own reasoning freely | proposed |

 647  | M161 | **The outbox: a row before a send, `retry_after` honoured exactly** | `queued → sent → confirmed`, with Telegram's `message_id` kept because editing later needs both ids. Per-chat pacing against ~1/s and the 20-per-minute group ceiling. A failed message is retried FROM ITS ROW: regenerating a digest an hour later produces a different digest, and the operator receives two letters that disagree. Plus `route` and `mute` as two switches — where a kind goes, and whether a source may raise it — because one per-project toggle collapses two questions into one; and a mute may **never** cover `refused` | proposed |
 648  | M162 | **Reply-to-answer — the operator's day moves to Telegram** | The bot posts a question; the operator replies to that message; `reply_to_message.message_id` identifies it exactly. **No parsing, no model, no ambiguity** — this is the finding that decided the build order, because the loop the operator described works with no provider at all. Plus the structured commands, because a command is a contract and a sentence is an interpretation | proposed |

 649  | M163 | **The evening letter, labelled with the window it covers** | Per project then total: finished, started, sessions and outcomes, questions asked and answered, decisions, what is blocked and for how long. Composed by arithmetic. **We cannot promise a time** — nothing runs when the laptop is closed — so a missed letter is sent late and SAYS it is late, and two missed days are two letters. A day with nothing in it gets a letter saying so, or the operator learns to read absence as quiet rather than as broken | proposed |
docs/evidence/backlog.md:652
 650  | M164 | **Notifiers are composed, not built** | `routine + agent + delivery + template`. Agents with their own instructions, schedules, triggers, agent-to-agent chains and gateway access ALL EXIST; the delivery step is the only new part, and composing means the notifier inherits the loop bound, the chain depth limit, the grant floor and the quota gate without one of them being rewritten. Created through a form today, a sentence when a provider lands — and the form is what the sentence would produce, not a placeholder for it | proposed |
 651  | M165 | **Capture, retention, and the consent that a work chat requires** | A bot cannot read history from before it joined, and reads a group at all only with privacy mode off — after which it receives EVERY message. So “analyse the thread” means analysing what we have been storing, which is a per-chat consent with a retention window, off by default. Built before the model tier because it is the expensive half and is useful alone as a record. A group is told less than a DM by default, and widening is an explicit journalled act | proposed |
 652  | M166 | **The CEO's core, and it is the whole CEO without a provider** | Hygiene, settlement by citation, criticality scoring and the digest arithmetic as pure modules in `shared/`, driven by the existing routine tick. **No loop, no framework, no key** — ADR-0038 decomposed the CEO and found four judgements among a dozen jobs, each of them a single typed transform rather than a goal pursued with tools. The product is complete at this step; everything after it is additive | proposed |
 653  | M167 | **A model port that may be ABSENT, and absence is a state rather than a crash** | A contract in `shared/` with a fake, so the core is tested against both a present and an absent provider. With none: the core runs unchanged, advisory calls are **skipped rather than stubbed** — a stub returns something, and something is what a person acts on — and the interface names which capabilities are unavailable instead of degrading quietly | proposed |
 654  | M168 | **The core is the checker, and a checker that never refuses is a finding** | Every advisory output is validated against facts before it reaches the Board: a proposed merge names two questions that exist and share a project, a drafted answer cites a fact that is current, a re-order moves within a stated band. **A refusal is journalled with its reason** — the stored verdict the orchestrator checklist asks for, and the evidence that lowers the CEO's trust level when a provider misbehaves | proposed |
 655  | M169 | **The provider router — and the three traps that are each a real bill** | Attempts capped **in total** rather than per provider, because three providers with three retries is nine calls for one prompt. An unhealthy provider probed **on a schedule**, because a check that only runs on failure never recovers and the chain runs one short with every request still succeeding. Failover **between turns only**, because reasoning carries a vendor credential and stripping it to move mid-turn is what produces the 400. Model, window and price at one boundary from configuration, never a table of vendor ids in source | proposed |
 656  | M170 | **The four typed calls, one at a time, each behind its validator** | `mergeSimilar`, `reorderWithReason`, `draftAnswer`, `readThread`. Each receives only what its transform needs and never the estate — a call handed everything has no window of its own. **None of the loop guards is implemented and that is a decision**: no tools, one turn, a typed value, so there is nothing to bound or trim. The day one of them gains a tool it becomes a loop and inherits every guard ADR-0038 declined | proposed |
 657  | M171 | **`model.called@1`, and the cost surface** | Provider, model, tokens and cost, journalled. The observable feed is then a view over a durable trace rather than the record itself — a stream nobody stored is a run that cannot be evaluated later. `quotaGate.ts` and `routine.ts` already prevent a CEO burning budget re-ranking a board nobody opened, so no new metering machinery is built | proposed |
docs/evidence/backlog.md:653
 651  | M165 | **Capture, retention, and the consent that a work chat requires** | A bot cannot read history from before it joined, and reads a group at all only with privacy mode off — after which it receives EVERY message. So “analyse the thread” means analysing what we have been storing, which is a per-chat consent with a retention window, off by default. Built before the model tier because it is the expensive half and is useful alone as a record. A group is told less than a DM by default, and widening is an explicit journalled act | proposed |
 652  | M166 | **The CEO's core, and it is the whole CEO without a provider** | Hygiene, settlement by citation, criticality scoring and the digest arithmetic as pure modules in `shared/`, driven by the existing routine tick. **No loop, no framework, no key** — ADR-0038 decomposed the CEO and found four judgements among a dozen jobs, each of them a single typed transform rather than a goal pursued with tools. The product is complete at this step; everything after it is additive | proposed |

 659  | M173 | **The decision graph shows movement, and who decided** | M144 shipped it as a list because there were no edges yet. The operator's ask — how the project reached here, human vs agent, and WHY an agent decided — needs no new storage: the decision fact carries `actor_kind` (person/agent/system), `superseded_by` carries the lineage, and the context lockfile (M49) carries `sha256` + `fact_ids` + `transcript_ids`, so “what did the agent know when it decided” is a JOIN, not a record.  661  | M175 | **The CEO is an agent — a small tool-calling loop, ours** | The operator chose flexibility and tools (ADR-0039, superseding ADR-0038 §1/§6). ~150 lines of `agent-orchestrator` §2 over the `ModelPort` router, with the four guards: in-loop trimming, wrap-up injection, a max-iteration guard, an iteration refund on recoverable errors. **Auditability moves from the shape of the run to the tools**: `settle_by_citation` refuses a basis-less settlement in the schema, and every tool call is journalled so the trajectory IS the audit trail. Dormant with no provider — the deterministic tools are the whole core | proposed |

The corpus and the trace already exist — every tool call the agent makes is journalled — so this reads real trajectories rather than inventing a fixture | proposed |

 661  | M175 | **The CEO is an agent — a small tool-calling loop, ours** | The operator chose flexibility and tools (ADR-0039, superseding ADR-0038 §1/§6). ~150 lines of `agent-orchestrator` §2 over the `ModelPort` router, with the four guards: in-loop trimming, wrap-up injection, a max-iteration guard, an iteration refund on recoverable errors. **Auditability moves from the shape of the run to the tools**: `settle_by_citation` refuses a basis-less settlement in the schema, and every tool call is journalled so the trajectory IS the audit trail.  662  | M176 | **A CEO trajectory eval, before its prompt is tuned** | The finding that orders the rest (`agent-harness`): with no evals every claim about an agent's behaviour is unfalsifiable, so the eval is the FIRST build step, ahead of the prompt. The corpus and the trace already exist — every tool call the agent makes is journalled — so this reads real trajectories rather than inventing a fixture | proposed |
 663  | M177 | **Close the cheap harness gaps found by the map** | Four things the audit surfaced, none waiting on a provider: inject **today's date** into the context pack (a model with a cutoff answers staleness from memory), **enumerate the status vocabulary** in the protocol so the agent stops learning the allowed set by rejection, **use `actor_kind` in trust** so an agent-authored fact is not read as a person-authored one (the column exists, unused), and make the protocol **re-assertable after a CLI compaction** that drops it | proposed |
 664  | M178 | **A heartbeat, because silence is ambiguous** | `fabric_heartbeat({ phase, note?, waiting_on? })` — a positive signal, journalled as `agent.heartbeat@1`. Liveness today is `idle` after 60s of silence, and silence hides three cases with different answers: an agent THINKING, an agent WEDGED, and an agent WAITING on a Board question — all silent. `phase` is enumerated (an agent invents its own vocabulary otherwise) and `waiting_on` names what a blocked agent waits on, turning silence into a fact. Five liveness states replace three, and only `stalled` claims a fault (ADR-0040) | proposed |

 665  | M179 | **The agent is watched from outside, because the reporter is what breaks** | A heartbeat is the agent's own account; the thing most likely broken is the reporter. Fabric watches the process (`onExit`), the heartbeat gap (a subtraction against a threshold), and **the absence of `context.read`** — every session is told to call `fabric_whoami` first and that call is journalled, so a session producing output that never read its rules is running WITHOUT the harness: a `harness.break@1` finding with the reason it can infer. Nothing surfaces this today | proposed |
 666  | M180 | **A failure is journalled with its KIND — “failed” is not one thing** | Runner-missing, skill-failed-to-load, harness-unreachable, agent-crashed, agent-wedged, task-failed-HONESTLY, advisory-model-failed, notifier-source-down — each a distinct detector and remedy, and an undeterminable kind is `unknown` and says so. M100's startup lesson generalised: name the precondition, or admit we do not know. A task that failed honestly is `review` with the answer “no”, NOT a crash — conflating them discards a real result | proposed |

 667  | M181 | **Degradation is stated, never silent** | A skill that breaks mid-run: the outcome carries “ran without skill X”. The model absent: the CEO runs its deterministic tools and names the advisory capabilities as unavailable. A notifier that cannot gather: it sends what it has with a partial note, or nothing with a recorded reason. A degradation the operator cannot see is a lie by omission — every path here states it. A `stalled` or `harness-break` agent enters the same Board queue as everything else (ADR-0040) | proposed |
docs/evidence/backlog.md:663
 661  | M175 | **The CEO is an agent — a small tool-calling loop, ours** | The operator chose flexibility and tools (ADR-0039, superseding ADR-0038 §1/§6). ~150 lines of `agent-orchestrator` §2 over the `ModelPort` router, with the four guards: in-loop trimming, wrap-up injection, a max-iteration guard, an iteration refund on recoverable errors. **Auditability moves from the shape of the run to the tools**: `settle_by_citation` refuses a basis-less settlement in the schema, and every tool call is journalled so the trajectory IS the audit trail. Dormant with no provider — the deterministic tools are the whole core | proposed |
 662  | M176 | **A CEO trajectory eval, before its prompt is tuned** | The finding that orders the rest (`agent-harness`): with no evals every claim about an agent's behaviour is unfalsifiable, so the eval is the FIRST build step, ahead of the prompt. The corpus and the trace already exist — every tool call the agent makes is journalled — so this reads real trajectories rather than inventing a fixture | proposed |
 663  | M177 | **Close the cheap harness gaps found by the map** | Four things the audit surfaced, none waiting on a provider: inject **today's date** into the context pack (a model with a cutoff answers staleness from memory), **enumerate the status vocabulary** in the protocol so the agent stops learning the allowed set by rejection, **use `actor_kind` in trust** so an agent-authored fact is not read as a person-authored one (the column exists, unused), and make the protocol **re-assertable after a CLI compaction** that drops it | proposed |
 664  | M178 | **A heartbeat, because silence is ambiguous** | `fabric_heartbeat({ phase, note?, waiting_on? })` — a positive signal, journalled as `agent.heartbeat@1`. Liveness today is `idle` after 60s of silence, and silence hides three cases with different answers: an agent THINKING, an agent WEDGED, and an agent WAITING on a Board question — all silent. `phase` is enumerated (an agent invents its own vocabulary otherwise) and `waiting_on` names what a blocked agent waits on, turning silence into a fact. Five liveness states replace three, and only `stalled` claims a fault (ADR-0040) | proposed |
 665  | M179 | **The agent is watched from outside, because the reporter is what breaks** | A heartbeat is the agent's own account; the thing most likely broken is the reporter. Fabric watches the process (`onExit`), the heartbeat gap (a subtraction against a threshold), and **the absence of `context.read`** — every session is told to call `fabric_whoami` first and that call is journalled, so a session producing output that never read its rules is running WITHOUT the harness: a `harness.break@1` finding with the reason it can infer. Nothing surfaces this today | proposed |
 666  | M180 | **A failure is journalled with its KIND — “failed” is not one thing** | Runner-missing, skill-failed-to-load, harness-unreachable, agent-crashed, agent-wedged, task-failed-HONESTLY, advisory-model-failed, notifier-source-down — each a distinct detector and remedy, and an undeterminable kind is `unknown` and says so. M100's startup lesson generalised: name the precondition, or admit we do not know. A task that failed honestly is `review` with the answer “no”, NOT a crash — conflating them discards a real result | proposed |

 668  | M182 | **An insight says what it is ABOUT — a closed category** | `category` on `memory_facts`: `project` (default) · `agents` · `harness` · `fabric` · `process`, closed with a CHECK because an open field grows four spellings of “fabric” in a week. Orthogonal to `kind`: a trap about the harness is `kind=trap, category=harness`. The three service categories are the raw material of Fabric's own product backlog, and today they drown in project memory where nobody sweeps across projects (ADR-0041) | proposed |

 674  | M188 | **Runs and plan steps — the unit of progress** | A run IS a session bound to a task (`task.session.attached@1` already journals the pair), so **every loop iteration is a whole run** — the operator's rule (paraphrased); a re-run starts fresh step statuses and history keeps every run. `fabric_plan_declare` + `fabric_plan_step` with ENUMERATED statuses (`planned·active·done·blocked·skipped·failed`), keyed to (task, run), journalled first, projection rebuildable. **A step status is a CLAIM and is rendered as one**; what Fabric observes (heartbeat, subtasks, blocks) is drawn as fact, never averaged (ADR-0042) | proposed |

 668  | M182 | **An insight says what it is ABOUT — a closed category** | `category` on `memory_facts`: `project` (default) · `agents` · `harness` · `fabric` · `process`, closed with a CHECK because an open field grows four spellings of “fabric” in a week. Orthogonal to `kind`: a trap about the harness is `kind=trap, category=harness`. The three service categories are the raw material of Fabric's own product backlog, and today they drown in project memory where nobody sweeps across projects (ADR-0041) | proposed |
 669  | M183 | **The anonymised product loop — service insights feed Fabric's backlog** | `fabric_feedback`: only `agents`/`harness`/`fabric` are EVER eligible — a `project` insight is **refused in the schema**, because its essence IS project data and no redaction makes it safe. A row carries the claim after M95 redaction + identifier stripping, the version, a WEEK-coarse date, and **no estate/project/actor/session — the columns do not exist**: anonymity by absence, not policy. Default ON, one journalled switch, and the pending queue VISIBLE on the harness screen. Upstream transport waits on an endpoint and is named as waiting | proposed |
 670  | M184 | **The retrospective is a cycle the CEO runs** | Weekly tick, scripts: merge exact duplicates (category+about), mirror service categories, count recurrences, detect dangling references. Judgement is PROPOSED, never exercised — a retired insight that was still true is worse than a stale one. **A recurring trap (N≥3, same about) becomes a Board question of kind `process`** — retro reaches the operator through the same ranked top-5, not a separate ceremony. Retirement is supersession; nothing silently deleted | proposed |
 671  | M185 | **One inbox on the dashboard — a view, not a store** | Two lanes over what already exists, split by one question: does this need you? “Needs you” = the Board's slice, can never be marked read; “happened” = notable events, expandable to payload/actor/project/deep-link, owing nothing. Every module's signals are already journal events or derived obligations; a notification store would be a second copy of the truth that drifts | proposed |
 672  | M186 | **The cycles view — everything that ticks, estate-wide** | Routine tick, chain advance, CEO hygiene, retro cycle, evening letter, board review: cadence, last run, next due, health — in ADR-0040's five-state vocabulary, because a cycle that silently stopped is a stalled agent one level up. M65's automations surface generalised from one project's routines to the estate's pulse | proposed |
 673  | M187 | **The density review — primary visible, the rest behind ONE disclosure primitive** | The operator's standing principle, applied as a sweep across every SCR: main data, main text, main meta, main controls visible; every additional control folds into a disclosure; the component set gains ONE disclosure primitive so screens fold the same way. Customisable disclosures (per-operator settings) deliberately AFTER the review proves where they are needed. Noted 2026-09-06, built as its own iteration | proposed |
 674  | M188 | **Runs and plan steps — the unit of progress** | A run IS a session bound to a task (`task.session.attached@1` already journals the pair), so **every loop iteration is a whole run** — the operator's rule (paraphrased); a re-run starts fresh step statuses and history keeps every run. `fabric_plan_declare` + `fabric_plan_step` with ENUMERATED statuses (`planned·active·done·blocked·skipped·failed`), keyed to (task, run), journalled first, projection rebuildable. **A step status is a CLAIM and is rendered as one**; what Fabric observes (heartbeat, subtasks, blocks) is drawn as fact, never averaged (ADR-0042) | proposed |
 675  | M189 | **The current-task widget — realtime progress, claims labelled as claims** | Preview on the agent tile and task page: run counter, plan steps with statuses, live heartbeat line, blocked-on named. Density principle: the main fraction plus a strip, everything else behind the open. Full run history is SCR-40's, not the widget's | proposed |

 678  | M194 | **The manager seat is a binding — built-in or an external agent** | `role=manager`, `provider_ref` = built-in / claude-code / … (ADR-0010/0021/0043).  679  | M195 | **Redaction at the agent surface — S1, HIGH** | `redact()` is applied to transcripts and NOT to what agents write through tools: a secret written into `memory_facts` becomes current, enters EVERY next context pack, and multiplies through transcripts; a question with a secret reaches the Board and (R3) Telegram. Fix at the surface boundary for every agent-written text field (memory claim, note, brief, question text/options), plus a probe planting a token in each field and watching the scrub, plus the mint-entropy check (S5) | **shipped** — and step 0 corrected the brief FOURFOLD: it named three fields in three tools; measuring found **thirteen across eight**, the most dangerous being `task.handoff@1`’s 8 000-char `value` — precisely the channel one agent uses to pass the next what it obtained. So the fix is ONE DOOR, not thirteen call sites (M106’s lesson one layer along): `appendRedacted` wraps every agent-written event, and all 15 raw appends in the surface now route through it. `redactPayload` walks the whole payload recursively — **no skip-list**, measured safe: nine ordinary values (ids, enums, `about` keys, formatted strings) came back byte-identical while the secret was caught. The count rides on the event, the transcript idiom. Token entropy verified at 192 bits (S5). Three defects planted, three caught |

 679  | M195 | **Redaction at the agent surface — S1, HIGH** | `redact()` is applied to transcripts and NOT to what agents write through tools: a secret written into `memory_facts` becomes current, enters EVERY next context pack, and multiplies through transcripts; a question with a secret reaches the Board and (R3) Telegram. Fix at the surface boundary for every agent-written text field (memory claim, note, brief, question text/options), plus a probe planting a token in each field and watching the scrub, plus the mint-entropy check (S5) | **shipped** — and step 0 corrected the brief FOURFOLD: it named three fields in three tools; measuring found **thirteen across eight**, the most dangerous being `task.handoff@1`’s 8 000-char `value` — precisely the channel one agent uses to pass the next what it obtained. So the fix is ONE DOOR, not thirteen call sites (M106’s lesson one layer along): `appendRedacted` wraps every agent-written event, and all 15 raw appends in the surface now route through it. `redactPayload` walks the whole payload recursively — **no skip-list**, measured safe: nine ordinary values (ids, enums, `about` keys, formatted strings) came back byte-identical while the secret was caught. The count rides on the event, the transcript idiom. Token entropy verified at 192 bits (S5). Three defects planted, three caught |
 680  | M196 | **Sandbox on the windows — S2** | All three BrowserWindows run `sandbox: false`; the preload uses only `contextBridge`, so sandbox compatibility is likely. Enable it, or record in the window comment exactly why it cannot be — an undocumented `sandbox: false` reads as an oversight forever | **shipped** — and the brief’s guess (“sandbox: true is likely compatible”) was wrong TWICE, both found by driving a real window. First: an ESM preload dies in a sandbox with `SyntaxError: Cannot use import statement outside a module` and `window.fabric` is **ABSENT** — the renderer loses all 27 namespaces silently, so the sandbox was off for a REASON nobody had recorded. Rebuilt as CJS, it failed a second way: in CJS format the resolver picks the **electron npm wrapper** — the one that `spawnSync`s the binary — dragging `child_process` into a context that has none. `external: ['electron']` is what makes the sandbox possible at all. Both halves are now held by a probe reading the SHIPPED bundle (CJS, no forbidden builtin, bridge still exposed, all 3 windows sandboxed); three regressions planted, three caught |

Rebuilt as CJS, it failed a second way: in CJS format the resolver picks the **electron npm wrapper** — the one that `spawnSync`s the binary — dragging `child_process` into a context that has none. `external: ['electron']` is what makes the sandbox possible at all. Both halves are now held by a probe reading the SHIPPED bundle (CJS, no forbidden builtin, bridge still exposed, all 3 windows sandboxed); three regressions planted, three caught |
 681  | M197 | **Двуязычие как храповик — RU/EN заложены, переводы докидываются** | The operator's decision 2026-09-06 (overturning the earlier EN-only): lay localisation NOW, top up translations later. The SYSTEM is the deliverable: `ru.ts` seeded with 93 hot-path keys per `docs/brand/locales/ru.md` («вы», length ×1.15, Fabric/PassionCode never translated, endonyms in the picker), and **the ratchet gate** — a new key lands in BOTH registries in the same change or the build fails; the 411-key legacy debt lives in `ru-baseline.txt`, which may only SHRINK (a translated key must be struck, a ghost row fails). Fallback to en is visible in the gate's count, never a raw key on screen. Four ratchet violations planted, four caught | **shipped** |
docs/evidence/backlog.md:677
 675  | M189 | **The current-task widget — realtime progress, claims labelled as claims** | Preview on the agent tile and task page: run counter, plan steps with statuses, live heartbeat line, blocked-on named. Density principle: the main fraction plus a strip, everything else behind the open. Full run history is SCR-40's, not the widget's | proposed |
 676  | M190 | **SCR-40 — the graphs screen: agent DID, project DID, plan SHOULD** | Three tabs, every graph a QUERY over existing edges (`task_links.rel`+`needs`, `origin_ref`, `question_blocks`, `assigned_by/to`, handoffs). DID graphs time-ordered from the journal — they cannot be wrong about the past; the SHOULD graph is the dependency DAG from goals and chains with progress overlay. **Two kinds, two renderings, never one with a toggle** — overlaying invites reading a plan as history. Previews on project/agent pages open here (ADR-0042) | proposed |
 677  | M191 | **The memory module made visible: pack preview, lineage, mirror drift** | The cheapest and most explanatory view: “what will the next agent be handed” compiles the pack DRY through the same code path and shows the facts chosen, the transcripts included, what the budget OMITTED and how close it is. Plus fact lineage on click (superseded chain, source session) and the declared-mirror drift state (“behind by N events”, ADR-0002). Zero new stores — readers only | proposed |
 678  | M194 | **The manager seat is a binding — built-in or an external agent** | `role=manager`, `provider_ref` = built-in / claude-code / … (ADR-0010/0021/0043). Fixed: the deterministic core, the checker, the schema floor, the trust ceiling, ONE manager tool surface. Selectable: only the judgement loop above the tools — every settlement by any seat passes the same floor into the same journal. Lifecycle by existing machinery: wake on tick/Board delta, verify by heartbeat + outside watch + trajectory eval, data in via the MANAGER PACK (lockfile journalled), data out ONLY through tools. The seat picker shows the cost difference | proposed |
 679  | M195 | **Redaction at the agent surface — S1, HIGH** | `redact()` is applied to transcripts and NOT to what agents write through tools: a secret written into `memory_facts` becomes current, enters EVERY next context pack, and multiplies through transcripts; a question with a secret reaches the Board and (R3) Telegram. Fix at the surface boundary for every agent-written text field (memory claim, note, brief, question text/options), plus a probe planting a token in each field and watching the scrub, plus the mint-entropy check (S5) | **shipped** — and step 0 corrected the brief FOURFOLD: it named three fields in three tools; measuring found **thirteen across eight**, the most dangerous being `task.handoff@1`’s 8 000-char `value` — precisely the channel one agent uses to pass the next what it obtained. So the fix is ONE DOOR, not thirteen call sites (M106’s lesson one layer along): `appendRedacted` wraps every agent-written event, and all 15 raw appends in the surface now route through it. `redactPayload` walks the whole payload recursively — **no skip-list**, measured safe: nine ordinary values (ids, enums, `about` keys, formatted strings) came back byte-identical while the secret was caught. The count rides on the event, the transcript idiom. Token entropy verified at 192 bits (S5). Three defects planted, three caught |

 682  | M198 | **A rebuild is not idempotent for `config_revision`** | Found by M97’s parity probe. The projector does `config_revision = config_revision + 1` on `project.updated@1` and `project.settings.updated@1`, so REPLAYING those events increments again — a rebuilt estate reports a different revision than the one it had. It arrived verbatim from migration 3 through every legacy copy, so it predates the refactor that found it, and the refactor journalled it rather than fixing it inside a diff that must contain no behaviour change. **P24 excludes the column and says why**, so the exclusion is a recorded finding rather than a convenience. Fix: derive the revision from the event count, or stop incrementing on replay | proposed |

docs/ux/flows.md:752
 750  - **Task analysis:**
 751    1. Expand the plan; goals show what is closed, running and next.
 752    2. Open the graph; follow an edge back to see how the state was reached.
 753    3. Follow forward to see what remains before the goal closes.
 754    4. Open a node; its task page opens.
 755    5. Read the orphan backlog; work nobody can justify is visible as a finding.
 756  - **Rejected shape:** a Gantt over dates — lost because nothing here has reliable dates, and a chart of invented dates reads as a plan.
 757  - **Flow:**
docs/ux/scenarios.md:1143
1141  - **Steps:**
1142    1. Operator expands the plan -> goals with their tasks, showing what is closed, what is running and who holds it.
1143    2. Operator opens the graph -> decisions on the left, tasks in the middle, goals on the right, with each node carrying its state and its holder.
1144    3. Operator follows an edge backwards -> how the current state was reached; forwards -> what remains before a goal closes.
1145    4. Operator looks for work with no goal -> it is listed separately, because a task nobody can justify is a finding rather than work.
1146    5. Operator draws an edge that would close a cycle -> it is refused at that moment, with the cycle named.
1147  - **Expected result:** the operator judges direction from the graph instead of inferring it from a list.
1148  - **Alt paths:** a goal with no tasks says it is undecomposed rather than complete; a project with no goals offers to derive them rather than showing an empty canvas.

docs/ux/vision.md:12
  10  ## 2. Core idea
  11
  12  Agent capability is abundant. Project-level coherence is scarce. PassionCode.ai bridges
  13  the gap by making the Project — its purpose, team, authority, work, Evidence and learning
  14  loop — the durable operating unit.
  15
